teen_social_life_blueprints prompts

Review every consequential prompt before model execution.

Support point 1: balanced complete response blueprint
Support point identifier: teen_social_life_blueprints_001

Survey context: Imagine a nationally representative 2026 survey of US teen girls. Predict the response distribution for the population named in the survey item. These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits, school, activities, relationships, and future outlook among US teen girls

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- social_media_hours: One to three hours
- posting_frequency: Less than once a week
- late_night_use: Never
- sleep_interference: Rarely
- social_comparison: Often
- connection_effect: Makes little difference
- parental_rules: Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Another platform
- school_effort: Rarely
- academic_confidence: Not at all confident
- homework_distraction: Often
- organized_activity: Less than once a week
- career_clarity: A very clear plan
- future_optimism: A little optimistic
- peer_belonging: Often

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a concise persona describing the attitudes that distinguish this response pattern. Address the persona in the
second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". This text will become a visible EDSL agent trait, so include
only the persona description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities. Then provide subjective response
probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to
1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media
- school_effort: Persistence and effort in schoolwork
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- academic_confidence: Confidence in ability to succeed academically
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all confident; 2. A little confident; 3. Somewhat confident; 4. Very confident
- homework_distraction: Social-media interference with homework
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- organized_activity: Participation in organized physical activity
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. One or two days a week; 4. Three or more days a week
- career_clarity: Clarity about a future career
  Options, in output order: 1. No clear idea; 2. A few vague ideas; 3. A fairly clear idea; 4. A very clear plan
- future_optimism: Optimism about personal future
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all optimistic; 2. A little optimistic; 3. Somewhat optimistic; 4. Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Sense of belonging among peers
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],
    "late_night_use": [numbers in option order],
    "sleep_interference": [numbers in option order],
    "social_comparison": [numbers in option order],
    "connection_effect": [numbers in option order],
    "parental_rules": [numbers in option order],
    "primary_platform": [numbers in option order],
    "school_effort": [numbers in option order],
    "academic_confidence": [numbers in option order],
    "homework_distraction": [numbers in option order],
    "organized_activity": [numbers in option order],
    "career_clarity": [numbers in option order],
    "future_optimism": [numbers in option order],
    "peer_belonging": [numbers in option order]
  }
}

Survey context: Imagine a nationally representative 2026 survey of US teen girls. Predict the response distribution for the population named in the survey item. These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits, school, activities, relationships, and future outlook among US teen girls

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- social_media_hours: Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: A few times a week
- late_night_use: Rarely
- sleep_interference: Often
- social_comparison: Sometimes
- connection_effect: Makes me feel less connected
- parental_rules: A few loose rules
- primary_platform: Instagram
- school_effort: Sometimes
- academic_confidence: Not at all confident
- homework_distraction: Sometimes
- organized_activity: Three or more days a week
- career_clarity: No clear idea
- future_optimism: Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Rarely

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a concise persona describing the attitudes that distinguish this response pattern. Address the persona in the
second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". This text will become a visible EDSL agent trait, so include
only the persona description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities. Then provide subjective response
probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to
1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media
- school_effort: Persistence and effort in schoolwork
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- academic_confidence: Confidence in ability to succeed academically
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all confident; 2. A little confident; 3. Somewhat confident; 4. Very confident
- homework_distraction: Social-media interference with homework
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- organized_activity: Participation in organized physical activity
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. One or two days a week; 4. Three or more days a week
- career_clarity: Clarity about a future career
  Options, in output order: 1. No clear idea; 2. A few vague ideas; 3. A fairly clear idea; 4. A very clear plan
- future_optimism: Optimism about personal future
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all optimistic; 2. A little optimistic; 3. Somewhat optimistic; 4. Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Sense of belonging among peers
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],
    "late_night_use": [numbers in option order],

Survey context: Imagine a nationally representative 2026 survey of US teen girls. Predict the response distribution for the population named in the survey item. These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits, school, activities, relationships, and future outlook among US teen girls

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- social_media_hours: Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Never or almost never
- late_night_use: Sometimes
- sleep_interference: Never
- social_comparison: Rarely
- connection_effect: Makes little difference
- parental_rules: Some clear rules
- primary_platform: Another platform
- school_effort: Never
- academic_confidence: A little confident
- homework_distraction: Sometimes
- organized_activity: Three or more days a week
- career_clarity: A few vague ideas
- future_optimism: Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Sometimes

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a concise persona describing the attitudes that distinguish this response pattern. Address the persona in the
second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". This text will become a visible EDSL agent trait, so include
only the persona description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities. Then provide subjective response
probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to
1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media
- school_effort: Persistence and effort in schoolwork
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- academic_confidence: Confidence in ability to succeed academically
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all confident; 2. A little confident; 3. Somewhat confident; 4. Very confident
- homework_distraction: Social-media interference with homework
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- organized_activity: Participation in organized physical activity
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. One or two days a week; 4. Three or more days a week
- career_clarity: Clarity about a future career
  Options, in output order: 1. No clear idea; 2. A few vague ideas; 3. A fairly clear idea; 4. A very clear plan
- future_optimism: Optimism about personal future
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all optimistic; 2. A little optimistic; 3. Somewhat optimistic; 4. Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Sense of belonging among peers
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],
    "late_night_use": [numbers in option order],
    "sleep_interference": [numbers in option order],

    "connection_effect": [numbers in option order],
    "parental_rules": [numbers in option order],
    "primary_platform": [numbers in option order],
    "school_effort": [numbers in option order],
    "academic_confidence": [numbers in option order],

Survey context: Imagine a nationally representative 2026 survey of US teen girls. Predict the response distribution for the population named in the survey item. These candidate questions were not observed in the American Teenagers Survey and are being used to study whether frontier-model consensus can supply explicitly synthetic calibration targets.
Battery topic: social-media habits, school, activities, relationships, and future outlook among US teen girls

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- social_media_hours: Less than one hour
- posting_frequency: Less than once a week
- late_night_use: Rarely
- sleep_interference: Never
- social_comparison: Rarely
- connection_effect: Makes me feel less connected
- parental_rules: Some clear rules
- primary_platform: Instagram
- school_effort: Often
- academic_confidence: Somewhat confident
- homework_distraction: Often
- organized_activity: Less than once a week
- career_clarity: A few vague ideas
- future_optimism: Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Never

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a concise persona describing the attitudes that distinguish this response pattern. Address the persona in the
second person, beginning with "Your views..." or "You...". This text will become a visible EDSL agent trait, so include
only the persona description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities. Then provide subjective response
probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order, contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to
1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- social_media_hours: Daily time on social media
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than one hour; 2. One to three hours; 3. Four or more hours
- posting_frequency: Posting or sharing frequency
  Options, in output order: 1. Never or almost never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. A few times a week; 4. At least once a day
- late_night_use: Social-media use after intended bedtime
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- sleep_interference: Social-media interference with sleep
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- social_comparison: Negative social comparison
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- connection_effect: Effect on connection to friends
  Options, in output order: 1. Makes me feel less connected; 2. Makes little difference; 3. Makes me feel more connected
- parental_rules: Strictness of parental social-media rules
  Options, in output order: 1. No rules; 2. A few loose rules; 3. Some clear rules; 4. Very strict rules
- primary_platform: Primary social-media platform
  Options, in output order: 1. TikTok; 2. Instagram; 3. YouTube; 4. Snapchat; 5. Another platform; 6. I do not use social media
- school_effort: Persistence and effort in schoolwork
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- academic_confidence: Confidence in ability to succeed academically
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all confident; 2. A little confident; 3. Somewhat confident; 4. Very confident
- homework_distraction: Social-media interference with homework
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often
- organized_activity: Participation in organized physical activity
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Less than once a week; 3. One or two days a week; 4. Three or more days a week
- career_clarity: Clarity about a future career
  Options, in output order: 1. No clear idea; 2. A few vague ideas; 3. A fairly clear idea; 4. A very clear plan
- future_optimism: Optimism about personal future
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all optimistic; 2. A little optimistic; 3. Somewhat optimistic; 4. Very optimistic
- peer_belonging: Sense of belonging among peers
  Options, in output order: 1. Never; 2. Rarely; 3. Sometimes; 4. Often

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "Your views on ...",
  "probabilities": {
    "social_media_hours": [numbers in option order],
    "posting_frequency": [numbers in option order],
    "late_night_use": [numbers in option order],
    "sleep_interference": [numbers in option order],
    "social_comparison": [numbers in option order],
    "connection_effect": [numbers in option order],
    "parental_rules": [numbers in option order],
    "primary_platform": [numbers in option order],
    "school_effort": [numbers in option order],
    "academic_confidence": [numbers in option order],
    "homework_distraction": [numbers in option order],
    "organized_activity": [numbers in option order],
    "career_clarity": [numbers in option order],
    "future_optimism": [numbers in option order],
    "peer_belonging": [numbers in option order]
  }
